```sql
                  hint = 'The payload names a column that does not exist on the server table.';
        end if;

        v_base_version := nullif(v_row ->> 'base_version', '')::int;

        if v_operation = 'insert' then
          -- Inserts require no existing row.
          execute format(
            'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
            v_table
          ) into v_existing using v_entity_id;

          if v_existing is not null then
            v_status := 'conflict';
            v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
            v_message := 'A row with this id already exists.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          else
            -- Server-owned columns are never taken from the client; the
            -- server_updated_at trigger stamps its own column.
            v_final := v_payload
              - 'server_updated_at' - 'version' - 'last_modified_by';
            if v_table = 'conflict_log' then
              -- The server column is a timestamptz defaulted to server now();
              -- the client's epoch-ms value must not be cast into it.
              v_final := v_final - 'created_at';
            end if;
            v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object(
              'version', 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
            );
            -- Only a table whose `created_at` is an epoch-ms *integer* column may
            -- receive the fallback: the six reference tables have none, and
            -- `conflict_log.created_at` is a server-owned timestamptz that must
            -- take its own default rather than the client's milliseconds.
            select exists (
              select 1
                from pg_catalog.pg_attribute a
                join pg_catalog.pg_class c on c.oid = a.attrelid
                join pg_catalog.pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
               where n.nspname = 'public'
                 and c.relname = v_table
                 and a.attname = 'created_at'
                 and a.attnum > 0
                 and a.atttypid in ('int8'::regtype, 'int4'::regtype)
            ) into v_has_created_at;
            if v_has_created_at and v_final ->> 'created_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('created_at', v_ms);
            end if;
            if v_final ->> 'updated_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('updated_at', v_ms);
            end if;
            -- `insert ... select *` reads NULL for an absent key rather than
            -- the column default, so a soft-delete flag must be explicit.
            if v_final ->> 'is_deleted' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('is_deleted', false);
            end if;

            if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, 'insert') then
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message := 'The caller may not create this row.';
            else
              -- Only the columns the snapshot carries are named, so a column
              -- the payload omitted still gets its own default rather than
              -- NULL (jsonb_populate_record does not apply defaults).
              select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                     string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                into v_cols, v_vals
                from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k;

              execute format(
                'insert into public.%I (%s) select %s from '
                'jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v',
                v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
              ) using v_final;
              execute format(
                'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                v_table
              ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
              v_status := 'accepted';
            end if;
          end if;
        else
          -- Lock the target row first, then decide.
          execute format(
            'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1 for update',
            v_table
          ) into v_existing using v_entity_id;

          if v_existing is null then
            v_status := 'rejected';
            v_error_code := 'not_found';
            v_message := 'The row no longer exists on the server.';
          elsif (v_existing ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean = true
                and v_operation = 'update' then
            v_status := 'tombstone';
            v_error_code := 'tombstone';
            v_message := 'The row was deleted on the server.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          elsif (v_existing ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean = true
                and v_operation = 'delete' then
            v_status := 'already_resolved';
            v_error_code := 'already_resolved';
            v_message := 'The row was already deleted.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;

          -- -----------------------------------------------------------------
          -- Delete-review approval (Task 20 Phase 4). The request row is the
          -- one locked above; the target is locked next.
          -- -----------------------------------------------------------------
          elsif v_table = 'delete_requests'
                and (v_payload ->> 'status') = 'approved' then
            v_req_status := coalesce(v_existing ->> 'status', '');

            if v_req_status in ('approved', 'rejected') then
              -- Already decided: nothing to do, and no second tombstone.
              v_status := 'already_resolved';
              v_error_code := 'already_resolved';
              v_message := 'This request was already reviewed.';
              v_new_row := v_existing;
            elsif not public.can_delete_firm(
                    nullif(v_existing ->> 'firm_id', '')::uuid) then
              -- The requester cannot approve their own request: only a member
              -- with delete permission may review one.
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message :=
                'Only a member with delete permission may review this request.';
            elsif v_base_version is null
                  or v_base_version <> (v_existing ->> 'version')::int then
              v_status := 'conflict';
              v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
              v_message := 'The request changed since this decision was made.';
              v_new_row := v_existing;
            else
              v_target_type := nullif(v_existing ->> 'entity_type', '');
              v_target_table := public.sync_entity_table(v_target_type);
              v_expected := nullif(v_existing ->> 'target_base_version', '')::int;

              if v_target_table is null or v_target_table not in (
                'cases', 'case_history', 'notes', 'case_references'
              ) then
                v_status := 'rejected';
                v_error_code := 'unsupported_target';
                v_message :=
                  'This request names a record that cannot be deleted here.';
              else
                execute format(
                  'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1 for update',
                  v_target_table
                ) into v_target using (v_existing ->> 'entity_id');

                if v_target is null
                   or coalesce((v_target ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean, false) then
                  -- Nothing left to approve. The request is untouched audit
                  -- history; the tombstone rides back for the reviewer.
                  v_status := 'already_resolved';
                  v_error_code := 'already_resolved';
                  v_message := 'The record was already deleted.';
                  v_new_row := v_existing;
                elsif coalesce(v_target ->> 'firm_id', '')
                      is distinct from coalesce(v_existing ->> 'firm_id', '') then
                  v_status := 'rejected';
                  v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
                  v_message := 'The record is not in this request''s firm.';
                elsif v_expected is null
                      or v_expected <> (v_target ->> 'version')::int then
                  -- The record moved under the request: park the request as
                  -- stale and adopt the version the reviewer just saw, so a
                  -- reconfirm re-decides against the row that exists now.
                  v_final := (v_existing || jsonb_build_object(
                      'status', 'stale_review_required',
                      'target_base_version', (v_target ->> 'version')::int,
                      'updated_at', v_ms,
                      'version', (v_existing ->> 'version')::int + 1,
                      'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
                    )) - 'server_updated_at';

                  if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, 'update') then
                    v_status := 'rejected';
                    v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
                    v_message := 'The caller may not write this row.';
                  else
                    select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                           string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                      into v_cols, v_vals
                      from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k
                     where k <> 'server_updated_at';

                    execute format(
                      'update public.%I t set (%s) = (%s) '
                      'from jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v '
                      'where t.id = $2',
                      v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
                    ) using v_final, v_entity_id;

                    execute format(
                      'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                      v_table
                    ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
                    v_status := 'target_conflict';
                    v_error_code := 'target_conflict';
                    v_message :=
                      'The record changed since this request was made.';
                  end if;
                else
                  -- Reconfirmed against the row that exists now: tombstone the
                  -- target in this same savepoint.
                  execute format(
                    'update public.%I t set is_deleted = true, '
                    '  version = coalesce(t.version, 1) + 1, '
                    '  last_modified_by = $1, updated_at = $2 '
                    'where t.id = $3 returning to_jsonb(t)',
                    v_target_table
                  ) using v_actor::text, v_ms, (v_existing ->> 'entity_id')
                  into v_target;

                  -- The same cascade an ordinary case delete performs, so an
                  -- approved request cannot leave live children behind. The
                  -- request stores the singular `entity_type` ('case'), so the
                  -- test is against the resolved *table* ('cases').
                  if v_target_table = 'cases' then
                    v_children := '[]'::jsonb;
                    for v_child in execute format(
                      'update public.case_history t '
                      'set is_deleted = true, '
                      '    version = coalesce(t.version, 1) + 1, '
                      '    last_modified_by = $1, '
                      '    updated_at = $2 '
                      'where t.case_id = $3 and t.is_deleted = false '
                      'returning to_jsonb(t)'
                    ) using v_actor::text, v_ms, (v_existing ->> 'entity_id')
                    loop
                      v_children := v_children || jsonb_build_array(v_child);
                    end loop;
                  end if;

                  -- Resolve the request beside the tombstone.
                  v_final := (v_existing || jsonb_build_object(
                      'status', 'approved',
                      'reviewed_by', v_actor::text,
                      'review_note', coalesce(
                        v_payload ->> 'review_note',
                        v_existing ->> 'review_note'),
                      'updated_at', v_ms,
                      'version', (v_existing ->> 'version')::int + 1,
                      'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
                    )) - 'server_updated_at';

                  if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, 'update') then
                    v_status := 'rejected';
                    v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
                    v_message := 'The caller may not write this row.';
                  else
                    select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                           string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                      into v_cols, v_vals
                      from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k
                     where k <> 'server_updated_at';

                    execute format(
                      'update public.%I t set (%s) = (%s) '
                      'from jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v '
                      'where t.id = $2',
                      v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
                    ) using v_final, v_entity_id;

                    execute format(
                      'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                      v_table
                    ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
                    v_status := 'accepted';
                  end if;
                end if;
              end if;
            end if;
          elsif v_base_version is null
                or v_base_version <> (v_existing ->> 'version')::int then
            v_status := 'conflict';
            v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
            v_message := 'The server version changed since this edit was made.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          else
            v_old_version := (v_existing ->> 'version')::int;
            v_final := (v_existing || v_payload)
              - 'server_updated_at' - 'version' - 'last_modified_by';
            if v_table = 'conflict_log' then
              v_final := v_final - 'created_at';
            end if;
            v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object(
              'version', v_old_version + 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
            );
            if v_operation = 'delete' then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('is_deleted', true);
            end if;
            if v_final ->> 'updated_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('updated_at', v_ms);
            end if;

            if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, v_operation) then
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message := 'The caller may not write this row.';
            else
              -- Update every column the final snapshot carries, except
              -- server_updated_at, which the trigger owns.
              -- Both lists are ordered by the same key, so the columns and
              -- their values can never fall out of step.
              select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                     string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                into v_cols, v_vals
                from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k
               where k <> 'server_updated_at';

              execute format(
                'update public.%I t set (%s) = (%s) '
                'from jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v '
                'where t.id = $2',
                v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
              ) using v_final, v_entity_id;

              execute format(
                'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                v_table
              ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
              v_status := 'accepted';
            end if;
          end if;
        end if;

        -- Atomic history and cascade soft-delete, only on an accepted case
        -- mutation. A delete-request approval whose target is a case performs
        -- its own cascade inside the branch above, so this stays case-only.
        if v_status = 'accepted' and v_table = 'cases' then
          if v_operation = 'delete' then
            -- One accepted case delete soft-deletes its children in this same
            -- savepoint; the changed children ride back to the caller.
            v_children := '[]'::jsonb;
            for v_child in execute format(
              'update public.case_history t '
              'set is_deleted = true, '
              '    version = coalesce(t.version, 1) + 1, '
              '    last_modified_by = $1, '
              '    updated_at = $2 '
              'where t.case_id = $3 and t.is_deleted = false '
              'returning to_jsonb(t)'
            ) using v_actor::text, v_ms, v_entity_id
            loop
              v_children := v_children || jsonb_build_array(v_child);
            end loop;
          elsif v_operation = 'update'
                and (v_final ->> 'next_date') is distinct from
                    (v_existing ->> 'next_date') then
            -- The client's provisional preview supplies its identity so the
            -- authoritative event replaces it one-for-one.
            v_prov := v_row -> 'provisional_history';
            if v_prov is null or jsonb_typeof(v_prov) <> 'object' then
              v_prov := '{}'::jsonb;
            end if;

            v_hist := v_prov || jsonb_build_object(
              'id', coalesce(v_prov ->> 'id', gen_random_uuid()::text),
              'case_id', v_entity_id,
              'user_id', v_new_row -> 'user_id',
              'firm_id', v_new_row -> 'firm_id',
              'source_mutation_id',
                coalesce(v_prov ->> 'source_mutation_id', v_mutation_id),
              'created_by', v_actor,
              'base_case_version',
                coalesce(nullif(v_prov ->> 'base_case_version', '')::int,
                         v_old_version),
              'result_case_version', (v_new_row ->> 'version')::int,
              'version', 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text,
              'is_deleted', false,
              'updated_at', v_ms
            );
            -- Nothing the client omitted is invented: the previous values come
            -- from the authoritative base row.
            if v_hist ->> 'hearing_date' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'hearing_date',
                coalesce(nullif(v_existing ->> 'next_date', '')::bigint, v_ms)
              );
            end if;
            if v_hist ->> 'last_step' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'last_step', v_existing -> 'last_step'
              );
            end if;
            if v_hist ->> 'note_result' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'note_result', v_existing -> 'notes_results'
              );
            end if;
            if v_hist ->> 'result' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'result', v_existing -> 'result'
              );
            end if;

            execute format(
              'insert into public.case_history select * from '
              'jsonb_populate_record(null::public.case_history, $1)'
            ) using v_hist;

            -- Hand the authoritative row back so the client can reconcile its
            -- provisional preview (same id) in one write.
            execute format(
              'select to_jsonb(t) from public.case_history t where t.id = $1'
            ) into v_hist_row using v_hist ->> 'id';
          end if;
        end if;
      end if;

      -- Terminal result for this mutation.
      if v_status = 'accepted' then
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'accepted',
          'version', (v_new_row ->> 'version')::int,
          'row', v_new_row,
          'children', coalesce(v_children, '[]'::jsonb),
          'history', v_hist_row,
          -- A delete-request approval carries the target it tombstoned, so the
          -- reviewer's client can apply the record's disappearance in one pass.
          'target', v_target,
          'target_entity_type', v_target_type
        );
      elsif v_status = 'target_conflict' then
        -- The request was parked as stale_review_required; hand back both rows
        -- so the reviewer can inspect the record that exists now.
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'target_conflict',
          'version', nullif(v_new_row ->> 'version', '')::int,
          'row', v_new_row,
          'target', v_target,
          'target_entity_type', v_target_type,
          'target_version', nullif(v_target ->> 'version', '')::int,
          'error_code', v_error_code,
          'message', v_message
        );
      elsif v_status in ('conflict', 'tombstone', 'already_resolved') then
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', v_status,
          'version', nullif(v_new_row ->> 'version', '')::int,
          'row', v_new_row,
          'target', v_target,
          'target_entity_type', v_target_type,
          'error_code', v_error_code,
          'message', v_message
        );
      else
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'rejected',
          'error_code', coalesce(v_error_code, 'validation_failed'),
          'message', coalesce(v_message, 'The mutation was refused.')
        );
      end if;
    exception
      when others then
        get stacked diagnostics
          v_state = returned_sqlstate,
          v_msg = message_text;
        -- A permanent, per-mutation failure is caught and reported; anything
        -- else is infrastructure and aborts the whole RPC.
        if v_state not in (
          '42501', 'P0001', '23502', '23503', '23505', '23514',
          '22P02', '22007', '22003', '42804', '42703'
        ) then
          raise;
        end if;
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'rejected',
          'error_code', case v_state
            when '42501' then 'permission_denied'
            when 'P0001' then 'entitlement_required'
            else 'validation_failed'
          end,
          'message', v_msg
        );
    end;

    -- Receipt for every terminal result, in the same transaction. It is written
    -- after the savepoint block so a per-mutation rejection still records one.
    insert into public.mutation_receipts (
      mutation_id, actor_id, entity_type, entity_id, request_hash,
      terminal_status, result_payload
    ) values (
      v_mutation_id, v_actor, v_entity_type, v_entity_id, v_hash,
      v_result ->> 'status', v_result
    )
    on conflict (mutation_id) do nothing;

    v_results[v_idx] := v_result;
  end loop;

  return jsonb_build_object('results', (
    select coalesce(jsonb_agg(v_results[k] order by k), '[]'::jsonb)
      from generate_subscripts(v_results, 1) as k
  ));
end;
$$;

comment on function public.push_changes(jsonb) is
  'Single atomic push pipeline: optimistic concurrency, per-mutation savepoints, idempotent receipts, authoritative case history and cascade soft-deletes, and the delete-review approval branch (locking, target_conflict/stale_review_required, already_resolved, atomic tombstone).';

-- Same surface as before: `anon` and `public` stay refused.
revoke all on function public.push_changes(jsonb) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.push_changes(jsonb) to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.330 — Task 20 Audit Closure — A Removed Member Cannot Merge A Write

### 0.0.330 · `20260930210000_sync_can_write_firm_membership.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-30.** Task 20 audit closure: `sync_can_write` again, so authorship of a firm-scoped row only counts while the row's firm is still the caller's firm (`firm_id = current_firm_id()`). Removing an associate relinks `profiles.firm_id` to NULL, but the author clause checked `user_id = auth.uid()` before any firm helper, so a removed member could still merge a queued **update** to a firm record they happened to author. The narrowing is applied to the same three places that trusted authorship: ordinary rows, custom references, and a requester's own pending delete request. A `firm_id`-less personal row is unaffected, and `can_edit_firm` / `can_delete_firm` still cover teammates and reviewers.

```sql
-- Task 20 audit closure — `sync_can_write` now requires firm membership for a
-- firm-scoped write, not authorship alone.
--
-- Phase 2 item 5 says removing an associate must "reject and never merge that
-- member's queued mutations". `remove_firm_member` relinks `profiles.firm_id` to
-- NULL, and EVERY guard in the schema was assumed to read `firm_id` afterwards.
-- They do — except one: `sync_can_write`'s author clause, `user_id = auth.uid()`,
-- which is checked *before* any firm helper. `cases`, `notes` and
-- `case_references` rows keep their author's `user_id` (that is the "preserve
-- immutable audit attribution" rule), so a removed member could still push an
-- **update** to a firm record they happened to author.
--
-- There were really **two** defects, and the second is why the first went
-- unnoticed. The author clause was only half the problem; the firm comparison
-- itself was written `firm_id = current_firm_id()`. Once removal makes
-- `current_firm_id()` NULL, that comparison evaluates to NULL, not false — and
-- `if not (<NULL>) then return false` does not fire in PL/pgSQL, because
-- `NOT NULL` is NULL and a NULL condition is treated as "do not branch". So the
-- guard was permissive in exactly the case it existed for: a caller with no firm
-- writing a row that has one. Both INSERT and UPDATE paths shared it.
--
-- The fix is therefore twofold, applied together:
--
--   * **`is not distinct from`** replaces `=` for every firm comparison. A row's
--     firm must be *the same non-null firm* as the caller's; a NULL on either side
--     is now a definite "no" rather than a slipped-through NULL. The
--     `firm_id is null` disjunct that follows still lets a personal row through.
--   * **authorship no longer outlives membership.** A firm-scoped row may be
--     written by its author only while that firm is still their firm
--     (`firm_id is not distinct from current_firm_id()`). The rule RLS has always
--     expressed — an author may edit their own row, even without `can_edit` —
--     is kept for current members; what changes is that it stops at removal. A
--     `firm_id`-less (personal) row is unaffected, so a removed member keeps
--     their own diary. `can_edit_firm(firm_id)` still covers a teammate editing
--     rows they did not author.
--
-- The same two corrections apply to the other places that trusted authorship:
-- a custom **reference** in a firm, and a requester's own **pending delete
-- request**, were both writable on `user_id` / `requested_by` alone. Reviews
-- (`can_delete`) are untouched.
--
-- Only `sync_can_write` changes; `push_changes` already calls it, so no RPC is
-- replaced. The function stays `stable security definer`.

create or replace function public.sync_can_write(
  p_table text,
  p_row jsonb,
  p_operation text
)
returns boolean
language plpgsql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_actor uuid := auth.uid();
  v_is_reference boolean := p_table in (
    'court_categories', 'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types',
    'on_behalfs'
  );
begin
  if v_actor is null then
    return false;
  end if;

  if p_operation = 'insert' then
    if p_table = 'delete_requests' then
      return coalesce((p_row ->> 'requested_by')::uuid, null) = v_actor
         and coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null)
             is not distinct from public.current_firm_id();
    elsif v_is_reference then
      return coalesce((p_row ->> 'is_custom')::boolean, false)
         and coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
         and (
           p_row ->> 'firm_id' is null
           or coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null)
                is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
         );
    else
      return coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
         and (
           p_row ->> 'firm_id' is null
           or coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null)
                is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
         );
    end if;
  end if;

  -- update / delete
  if p_table = 'delete_requests' then
    -- The requester may edit or withdraw their own request while it is pending,
    -- but only while the request's firm is still theirs: a removed requester must
    -- not be able to touch it. A member with delete permission may still review.
    return (
      coalesce((p_row ->> 'requested_by')::uuid, null) = v_actor
      and (p_row ->> 'status') = 'pending'
      and coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null)
            is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
    ) or public.can_delete_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null));
  elsif v_is_reference then
    -- A built-in row has no creator and no firm; only a custom row is writable.
    if not coalesce((p_row ->> 'is_custom')::boolean, false) then
      return false;
    end if;
    return (
        coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
        and (
          p_row ->> 'firm_id' is null
          or coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null)
               is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
        )
      )
      or public.can_edit_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null));
  elsif p_table = 'conflict_log' then
    return coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor;
  else
    -- Authorship only counts while the row's firm is still the caller's firm: a
    -- removed member keeps their own personal rows but not the firm's.
    if not (
      (
        coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
        and (
          p_row ->> 'firm_id' is null
          or coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null)
               is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
        )
      )
      or public.can_edit_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null))
    ) then
      return false;
    end if;
    -- The delete transition additionally needs can_delete, mirroring
    -- enforce_delete_transition().
    if p_operation = 'delete' and (p_row ->> 'firm_id') is not null then
      return public.can_delete_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null));
    end if;
    return true;
  end if;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.sync_can_write(text, jsonb, text) is
  'Explicit write re-check for push_changes: same rules as the Phase 4 RLS policies, evaluated on the row that would be written. A firm comparison uses `is not distinct from`, because `=` against a NULL current_firm_id would yield NULL and slip past an `if not`. Authorship of a firm-scoped row is honoured only while the row''s firm is still the caller''s firm, so a removed member cannot merge a queued write.';
```

## App Version 0.0.332 — Task 20 Audit Follow-Up — The Policies Refuse The Same Write As The RPC

### 0.0.332 · `20260930220000_rls_firm_membership.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-30.** Task 20 audit follow-up: the same membership rule, applied to RLS. `sync_can_write` closed the *app* path in 0.0.330, but the table policies still said `user_id = auth.uid()` on a firm-scoped row, so a removed associate could still write their authored firm rows through PostgREST directly — rewriting one, or detaching it from the firm by nulling `firm_id`. Every INSERT and UPDATE policy that trusts authorship now requires the row to be personal (`firm_id is null`) or still the caller's firm (`is not distinct from current_firm_id()`), across the ten tables that share one shape plus `conflict_log` and the requester's own pending `delete_requests` row. SELECT policies and table privileges are deliberately untouched.

```sql
-- Task 20 follow-up (RLS) — authorship of a firm-scoped row is a membership
-- claim, not a permanent one.
--
-- The 0.0.330 migration closed this hole in `sync_can_write`, which is the path
-- the app itself uses. It could not close it in the *policies*, and the audit
-- left that open deliberately rather than silently: a table policy still said
-- `user_id = auth.uid()` on a firm-scoped row, so a removed associate — whose
-- authored rows keep their `user_id`, because attribution is immutable — could
-- still write those rows straight through PostgREST, without the sync RPC ever
-- being consulted. It is not a theoretical gap: `cases_update_scoped`'s `with
-- check` asked only that the NEW row keep `user_id = auth.uid()`, so a removed
-- member could rewrite a firm record or move it out of the firm by nulling
-- `firm_id`.
--
-- This migration makes the policies say what `sync_can_write` now says, in the
-- same places that trust authorship:
--
--   * ordinary push/pull rows — cases, case_history, notes, case_references;
--   * the six editable reference tables — only the author clause moves, the
--     `is_custom` handling stays exactly as Task 16 wrote it;
--   * a requester's own still-pending `delete_requests` row, which is the one
--     place the author column is named `requested_by`;
--   * `conflict_log`, whose policies were already own-rows-only and gain the
--     membership condition and nothing else.
--
-- Three deliberate limits, so this stays a security fix and not a redesign:
---   1. **Only INSERT and UPDATE policies change.** The SELECT policies compare
--      the row's firm to the caller's with a plain equality, and a NULL on
--      either side makes that comparison NULL — which is not true, so a caller
--      with no firm already reads nothing from a firm row. `is not distinct
--      from` would be exactly equivalent there; rewriting a read policy for no
--      behavioural gain is risk without reward.
--   2. **Privileges are untouched.** The client writes these tables only through
--      `push_changes` (a `security definer` function, so it runs with the
--      migration owner's privileges and is unaffected by any grant the
--      `authenticated` role holds), while the pull is SELECT-only. Revoking
--      INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE from `authenticated` would close the direct path
--      too, but it was put to the user and declined in favour of fixing the
--      policies themselves — so the direct path is now refused *by policy*.
--   3. **A personal row (`firm_id is null`) stays entirely its author's**, before
--      and after removal. That is the individual diary AGENTS.md rule 3
--      protects: an associate who leaves a firm keeps it, and the firm never
--      gains a claim on it.
--
-- `can_edit_firm` / `can_delete_firm` are unchanged and still cover teammates
-- and reviewers; their NULL-safe `p_firm_id is not null` guard is what makes the
-- disjunct harmless for a caller with no firm.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- The ten tables that share one author-clause shape.
--
-- `cases`, `case_history`, `notes` and `case_references` are the case-data half
-- of the push/pull domain; the six reference tables are the editable
-- configuration half. Both halves name the same policy patterns
-- (`<table>_insert_own`, `<table>_update_scoped`), so one loop keeps the twelve
-- spellings from drifting apart.
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

do $$
declare
  v_table text;
  v_tables text[] := array[
    'cases', 'case_history', 'notes', 'case_references',
    'court_categories', 'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types',
    'on_behalfs'
  ];
begin
  foreach v_table in array v_tables loop
    -- INSERT: as yourself, into your own firm or into no firm at all.
    execute format(
      'drop policy if exists %1$s_insert_own on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create policy %1$s_insert_own on public.%1$I for insert to '
      'authenticated with check (user_id = auth.uid() and (firm_id is null or '
      'firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()))',
      v_table
    );

    -- UPDATE: your own row while its firm is still your firm, or a firm member
    -- with `can_edit`. USING and WITH CHECK say the same thing, which is what
    -- stops a row being updated *into* a state its writer could not have
    -- created — the previous asymmetry let a removed author detach a firm row
    -- by nulling `firm_id`.
    execute format(
      'drop policy if exists %1$s_update_scoped on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create policy %1$s_update_scoped on public.%1$I for update to '
      'authenticated using ((user_id = auth.uid() and (firm_id is null or '
      'firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id())) or '
      'public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)) with check ((user_id = auth.uid() and '
      '(firm_id is null or firm_id is not distinct from '
      'public.current_firm_id())) or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id))',
      v_table
    );
  end loop;
end;
$$;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- conflict_log — own rows only, and now only while that authorship is current.
--
-- No `can_edit_firm` disjunct, exactly as before: a conflict log is a device's
-- own record of a merge it performed (the mirror exists so the same conflict can
-- be read on another device), and a firm member has no business writing a
-- colleague's. The policies were already `user_id = auth.uid()` on both sides
-- and change only by gaining the membership condition.
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists conflict_log_insert_own on public.conflict_log;
create policy conflict_log_insert_own on public.conflict_log
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id())
  );

drop policy if exists conflict_log_update_own on public.conflict_log;
create policy conflict_log_update_own on public.conflict_log
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id())
  )
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id())
  );

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- delete_requests — the requester's own still-pending request.
--
-- A request is always firm-scoped (the insert policy has never allowed a
-- `firm_id`-less one), so there is no personal-row case here and every firm
-- comparison is `is not distinct from`. The requester may refresh the snapshot
-- or withdraw the request while it is pending *and* the firm is still theirs; a
-- reviewer with `can_delete` decides it. Nothing else is writable, and the
-- `with check` deliberately does not repeat `status = 'pending'`: a decision
-- moves the row out of that state, and only the reviewer's disjunct may do it.
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists delete_requests_insert_firm on public.delete_requests;
create policy delete_requests_insert_firm on public.delete_requests
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    requested_by = auth.uid()
    and firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
  );

drop policy if exists delete_requests_update_scoped on public.delete_requests;
create policy delete_requests_update_scoped on public.delete_requests
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    (
      requested_by = auth.uid()
      and status = 'pending'
      and firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
    )
    or public.can_delete_firm(firm_id)
  )
  with check (
    (
      requested_by = auth.uid()
      and firm_id is not distinct from public.current_firm_id()
    )
    or public.can_delete_firm(firm_id)
  );
```

## App Version 0.0.333 — Task 21 Phase 1 — The MFS Configuration Table

### 0.0.333 · `20261001000000_payment_configuration.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-01.** Task 21 Phase 1's payments configuration: a read-only server table (`provider`, `receiving_number`, `is_active`, `updated_at`) that holds the MFS receiving numbers, authenticated SELECT only and no client write policy at all. It exists so a receiving number can rotate without a release (Rev 2.4 §5); it is not seeded, and the client falls back to validated build defaults until an admin inserts a row.

```sql
-- Task 21 Phase 1 — the MFS receiving numbers are server configuration, not
-- build constants.
--
-- Task 16 shipped `payment_submissions` and the admin `approve_payment` RPC, but
-- nothing that holds *where* a user sends money. The Addendum (Rev 2.4 §5)
-- requires the payments configuration service to take its numbers from
-- "validated build environment or authenticated remote configuration" so a
-- number can rotate without a release. Build-time defaults can bootstrap a
-- build, but a release is the wrong unit of change for a receiving number: the
-- number belongs to the firm's MFS account and can change at any moment.
--
-- This migration is the remote half. It stores one row per supported provider
-- (`bkash`, `nagad`, `rocket`), readable by any authenticated client and
-- writable by no client at all:
--
--   * SELECT policy for `authenticated`: the numbers are meant to be shown to
--     the user, so they are readable; they are not secret.
--   * No INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy: a receiving number is set by an admin
--     (a controlled migration or a service-role/Studio operation), never by the
--     app. `anon` reads nothing: RLS is on and only `authenticated` has a policy,
--     so the default table grant is not a read path.
--   * `receiving_number <> ''` and the provider allow-list keep a malformed row
--     from ever being created, so the client's validation only has to defend
--     against a genuinely old/absent row, not against a bad one.
--
-- The table is intentionally **not seeded**: the real bKash/Nagad/Rocket numbers
-- are not invented here. An empty table is a valid state and the service falls
-- back to its validated build defaults (`--dart-define`); once an admin inserts
-- or updates a row, the remote value is authoritative and rotates the number
-- without a release.

create table if not exists public.payment_configuration (
  provider text primary key
    check (provider in ('bkash', 'nagad', 'rocket')),
  receiving_number text not null
    check (receiving_number <> ''),
  is_active boolean not null default true,
  updated_at timestamptz not null default now()
);

alter table public.payment_configuration enable row level security;

-- Read-only to authenticated clients: display-only data that rotates centrally.
drop policy if exists payment_configuration_select_authenticated
  on public.payment_configuration;
create policy payment_configuration_select_authenticated
  on public.payment_configuration
  for select to authenticated
  using (true);

-- No write policies on purpose: this is server-managed configuration. The
-- absence of a policy is the rule, not an omission — the same shape as the
-- server-internal ledgers.
```

## App Version 0.0.339 — Task 21 Follow-Up — Four Plans And The Receiving Number

### 0.0.339 · `20261001010000_payment_plans_four.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-01.** Task 21 follow-up's four-plan approval: replaces `approve_payment` so the subscription knows `monthly`, `quarterly`, `half_yearly` and `annual`, validates ৳100/৳285/৳550/৳1,000 and extends by 1/3/6/12 months — the admin gate, lock, idempotency, firm mirror and audit event are unchanged.

```sql
-- Task 21 follow-up — the subscription sells four durations, not two.
--
-- The paywall now offers 1 Month (৳100), 3 Months (৳285), 6 Months (৳550) and
-- 1 Year (৳1,000). `approve_payment` from `20260929102200` knew only `monthly`
-- and `annual` and extended by a month or a year, so a submission for one of the
-- two new plans would have been refused `invalid_plan` and could never be
-- approved. This migration replaces the function with the four-plan version.
--
-- `plan_type` is plain `text` on `payment_submissions` (there is no check
-- constraint to alter), so the only server change is the function body: the
-- accepted keys, the amount each key must carry, and the interval each key
-- extends by. Everything else — the admin gate, the `FOR UPDATE` lock, the
-- idempotency, the owner-firm mirror and the single immutable audit event — is
-- unchanged; only the four plan facts move.
--
-- Table class: `payment_submissions` and `payment_approval_events` remain
-- server-managed (no client write policy exists for either).

create or replace function public.approve_payment(payment_submission_id uuid)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- Configured plan prices, in BDT. Kept here as named constants so a price
  -- change is one migration, and so the RPC validates the amount the server
  -- believes rather than the amount the client sent.
  c_monthly_bdt constant integer := 100;
  c_quarterly_bdt constant integer := 285;
  c_half_yearly_bdt constant integer := 550;
  c_annual_bdt constant integer := 1000;

  v_submission public.payment_submissions%rowtype;
  v_profile public.profiles%rowtype;
  v_event public.payment_approval_events%rowtype;
  v_previous_expiry timestamptz;
  v_new_expiry timestamptz;
begin
  -- A GRANT to `authenticated` is not authorization: every caller shares that
  -- database role, so the registry itself decides.
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Missing rows return a status without revealing anything about other users.
  select * into v_submission
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.id = payment_submission_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- The parameter has the same name as the column, and `payment_approval_events`
  -- has that column, so the reference must be qualified: unqualified it is
  -- ambiguous and the function fails with "It could refer to either a PL/pgSQL
  -- variable or a table column" the first time it is called.
  select * into v_event
    from public.payment_approval_events e
   where e.payment_submission_id = approve_payment.payment_submission_id;

  -- Idempotent retries: a duplicate call can never extend expiry twice.
  if v_submission.status = 'approved' then
    if found then
      return jsonb_build_object(
        'status', 'already_approved',
        'new_expiry', v_event.new_expiry
      );
    end if;
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'already_resolved');
  end if;

  if v_submission.status <> 'pending' then
    -- A rejected submission needs a separately audited reset; approval is not
    -- a back door around that.
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'already_resolved');
  end if;

  if v_submission.plan_type not in (
    'monthly', 'quarterly', 'half_yearly', 'annual'
  ) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_plan');
  end if;

  -- The CASE is parenthesized because plpgsql reads an IF condition up to the
  -- first top-level `then`; an unwrapped CASE ends it at its own `when ... then`
  -- and the function then fails to compile with "syntax error at end of input".
  if v_submission.amount <> (case v_submission.plan_type
                               when 'monthly' then c_monthly_bdt
                               when 'quarterly' then c_quarterly_bdt
                               when 'half_yearly' then c_half_yearly_bdt
                               else c_annual_bdt
                             end) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_amount');
  end if;

  select * into v_profile
    from public.profiles p
   where p.id = v_submission.user_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- Server time decides the base; a lapsed-but-not-expired window is not lost.
  -- The interval is the plan's own duration, so it can never disagree with the
  -- amount validated above.
  v_previous_expiry := v_profile.subscription_expiry;
  v_new_expiry := greatest(coalesce(v_previous_expiry, now()), now())
    + case v_submission.plan_type
        when 'monthly' then interval '1 month'
        when 'quarterly' then interval '3 months'
        when 'half_yearly' then interval '6 months'
        else interval '1 year'
      end;

  update public.profiles p
     set subscription_expiry = v_new_expiry,
         subscription_status = 'premium'
   where p.id = v_submission.user_id;

  update public.payment_submissions s
     set status = 'approved',
         approved_at = now(),
         approved_by = auth.uid(),
         updated_at = (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint
   where s.id = payment_submission_id;

  -- Exactly one immutable event per submission (UNIQUE on the FK).
  insert into public.payment_approval_events (
    payment_submission_id, user_id, approved_by, plan_type, amount,
    previous_expiry, new_expiry
  )
  values (
    payment_submission_id, v_submission.user_id, auth.uid(),
    v_submission.plan_type, v_submission.amount,
    v_previous_expiry, v_new_expiry
  );

  return jsonb_build_object(
    'status', 'approved',
    'previous_expiry', v_previous_expiry,
    'new_expiry', v_new_expiry
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.approve_payment(uuid) is
  'Admin-only atomic MFS approval: lock, validate, extend entitlement (1/3/6/12 months), mirror to the firm, one audit event.';

-- The owner must not be reachable by ordinary clients, and the grant is for the
-- shared `authenticated` role only (the app_admins check above is the real gate).
-- `from public` is not enough on Supabase: its default privileges grant EXECUTE
-- on every new function in `public` *directly* to `anon` and `authenticated`,
-- and a direct grant outlives a revoke aimed at PUBLIC.
revoke all on function public.approve_payment(uuid) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.approve_payment(uuid) to authenticated;
```

### 0.0.339 · `20261001020000_seed_payment_configuration.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-01.** Task 21 follow-up's seeded receiving numbers: one idempotent row per provider (`bkash`, `nagad`, `rocket`) in `payment_configuration` with the firm's single MFS account in local 11-digit form, so the checkout shows a real number instead of falling back to build defaults.

```sql
-- Task 21 follow-up — the real MFS receiving number is now known.
--
-- `20261001000000_payment_configuration.sql` created the read-only
-- `payment_configuration` table and deliberately left it **unseeded**, because
-- the real bKash/Nagad/Rocket number was not invented. The firm's actual
-- account is now known, so this migration seeds one row per provider with the
-- same number — the single receiving account for all three channels — through
-- an idempotent upsert so re-running cannot duplicate or half-write it.
--
-- The number is stored in the local 11-digit form the client validates
-- (`isValidBdMobileNumber`: `01` + operator digit + 8 digits); the international
-- `+880…` form would be rejected by that rule and shown to no one.
--
-- Table class: `payment_configuration` is server-managed configuration — still
-- readable by any authenticated client and writable by no client at all. This
-- migration is the controlled admin write that the table's missing write policy
-- reserves for exactly this.

insert into public.payment_configuration (provider, receiving_number)
values
  ('bkash', '01835252721'),
  ('nagad', '01835252721'),
  ('rocket', '01835252721')
on conflict (provider) do update
  set receiving_number = excluded.receiving_number,
      is_active = true,
      updated_at = now();
```

## App Version 0.0.349 — Task 21 Follow-Up — Discounted Six-Month And Annual Plans

### 0.0.349 · `20261002100000_payment_prices_discounted.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-02.** Discounted 6 Months and 1 Year plans: replaces `approve_payment` so it validates ৳100/৳285/৳540/৳960 — the two longer plans priced against the ৳100 monthly rate (10% and 20% off) instead of ৳550 and ৳1,000. Only the four price constants move; the gate, lock, idempotency, intervals, firm mirror and audit event are unchanged.

```sql
-- Task 21 follow-up — the 6 Months and 1 Year plans are now discounted.
--
-- `20261001010000_payment_plans_four.sql` replaced `approve_payment` with the
-- four-plan version, whose four price constants are the only place the server
-- decides what a plan may cost. Two of them are now wrong: a submission for
-- ৳540 or ৳960 would be refused `invalid_amount` and could never be approved,
-- while the paywall and the checkout both quote the new amounts.
--
-- The discount is priced against the same per-month rate the paywall strikes
-- through — ৳100 a month — so 6 months is 10% off ৳600 and a year is 20% off
-- ৳1,200. The 3 Months plan keeps ৳285, already 5% off ৳300, and is unchanged.
--
-- Only the four constants move. The admin gate, the `FOR UPDATE` lock, the
-- idempotency, the intervals each plan extends by, the owner-firm mirror and
-- the single immutable audit event are all identical, so the whole change is
-- this one `create or replace`.
--
-- Table class: `payment_submissions` and `payment_approval_events` remain
-- server-managed (no client write policy exists for either), and this runs as
-- the controlled admin write that re-issuing a function under its old grants
-- requires.

create or replace function public.approve_payment(payment_submission_id uuid)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- Configured plan prices, in BDT. Kept here as named constants so a price
  -- change is one migration, and so the RPC validates the amount the server
  -- believes rather than the amount the client sent. The 6 Months and 1 Year
  -- plans are discounted against the ৳100 monthly rate: 10% off ৳600 and 20%
  -- off ৳1,200.
  c_monthly_bdt constant integer := 100;
  c_quarterly_bdt constant integer := 285;
  c_half_yearly_bdt constant integer := 540;
  c_annual_bdt constant integer := 960;

  v_submission public.payment_submissions%rowtype;
  v_profile public.profiles%rowtype;
  v_event public.payment_approval_events%rowtype;
  v_previous_expiry timestamptz;
  v_new_expiry timestamptz;
begin
  -- A GRANT to `authenticated` is not authorization: every caller shares that
  -- database role, so the registry itself decides.
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Missing rows return a status without revealing anything about other users.
  select * into v_submission
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.id = payment_submission_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- The parameter has the same name as the column, and `payment_approval_events`
  -- has that column, so the reference must be qualified: unqualified it is
  -- ambiguous and the function fails with "It could refer to either a PL/pgSQL
  -- variable or a table column" the first time it is called.
  select * into v_event
    from public.payment_approval_events e
   where e.payment_submission_id = approve_payment.payment_submission_id;

  -- Idempotent retries: a duplicate call can never extend expiry twice.
  if v_submission.status = 'approved' then
    if found then
      return jsonb_build_object(
        'status', 'already_approved',
        'new_expiry', v_event.new_expiry
      );
    end if;
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'already_resolved');
  end if;

  if v_submission.status <> 'pending' then
    -- A rejected submission needs a separately audited reset; approval is not
    -- a back door around that.
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'already_resolved');
  end if;

  if v_submission.plan_type not in (
    'monthly', 'quarterly', 'half_yearly', 'annual'
  ) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_plan');
  end if;

  -- The CASE is parenthesized because plpgsql reads an IF condition up to the
  -- first top-level `then`; an unwrapped CASE ends it at its own `when ... then`
  -- and the function then fails to compile with "syntax error at end of input".
  if v_submission.amount <> (case v_submission.plan_type
                               when 'monthly' then c_monthly_bdt
                               when 'quarterly' then c_quarterly_bdt
                               when 'half_yearly' then c_half_yearly_bdt
                               else c_annual_bdt
                             end) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_amount');
  end if;

  select * into v_profile
    from public.profiles p
   where p.id = v_submission.user_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- Server time decides the base; a lapsed-but-not-expired window is not lost.
  -- The interval is the plan's own duration, so it can never disagree with the
  -- amount validated above.
  v_previous_expiry := v_profile.subscription_expiry;
  v_new_expiry := greatest(coalesce(v_previous_expiry, now()), now())
    + case v_submission.plan_type
        when 'monthly' then interval '1 month'
        when 'quarterly' then interval '3 months'
        when 'half_yearly' then interval '6 months'
        else interval '1 year'
      end;

  update public.profiles p
     set subscription_expiry = v_new_expiry,
         subscription_status = 'premium'
   where p.id = v_submission.user_id;

  update public.payment_submissions s
     set status = 'approved',
         approved_at = now(),
         approved_by = auth.uid(),
         updated_at = (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint
   where s.id = payment_submission_id;

  -- Exactly one immutable event per submission (UNIQUE on the FK).
  insert into public.payment_approval_events (
    payment_submission_id, user_id, approved_by, plan_type, amount,
    previous_expiry, new_expiry
  )
  values (
    payment_submission_id, v_submission.user_id, auth.uid(),
    v_submission.plan_type, v_submission.amount,
    v_previous_expiry, v_new_expiry
  );

  return jsonb_build_object(
    'status', 'approved',
    'previous_expiry', v_previous_expiry,
    'new_expiry', v_new_expiry
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.approve_payment(uuid) is
  'Admin-only atomic MFS approval: lock, validate, extend entitlement (1/3/6/12 months), mirror to the firm, one audit event.';

-- `create or replace` drops the old grants, and on Supabase the default
-- privileges would re-grant EXECUTE to `anon` and `authenticated` directly —
-- a direct grant outlives a revoke aimed at PUBLIC, so both are re-asserted.
revoke all on function public.approve_payment(uuid) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.approve_payment(uuid) to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.354 — Task 23 Phase 1 — The Admin Panel On The Server

### 0.0.354 · `20261002110000_admin_panel.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-02.** Task 23 Admin Panel: `app_admins` gains hand-filled `full_name` and `email`, `payment_submissions` gains `rejected_by`/`rejected_at`, and three admin-gated RPCs arrive — `is_app_admin()` (the only way a client learns its own status), `admin_list_payment_submissions()` (every submission joined to the payer profile) and `admin_reject_payment()`. Approval needs no new code; `profiles` stays self-or-firm.

```sql
-- Task 23 — the Admin Panel: who may administer, and the two calls it makes.
--
-- `app_admins` shipped empty with RLS on and **no policy of any kind**, so the
-- app cannot read it — which is correct: it is the registry, and the registry is
-- not the question the app asks. The app asks "am I an admin?", so this adds
-- `is_app_admin()` to answer exactly that for the caller and nothing else.
--
-- The panel then needs two more things it cannot get today. It cannot list
-- anyone's payments (`payment_submissions_select_own` is `user_id = auth.uid()`
-- and there is no admin branch), and it cannot learn who paid: a submission
-- carries only a `user_id`, and `profiles_select_self_or_firm` shows a client
-- its own row or its firm's. Both are answered by one admin-gated RPC that
-- joins the two, rather than by opening `profiles` to every profile in the
-- system — a much wider door than the panel needs.
--
-- Approval needs nothing: `approve_payment` already exists, is already
-- admin-gated and already writes its own immutable audit event. Rejection does
-- not exist, so this adds it — with the who and the when recorded, because a
-- rejection that leaves no trace of who made it is not an audit trail.
--
-- Table class: `app_admins` stays server-managed. Adding two descriptive
-- columns changes nothing about who may read or write it, and they are
-- hand-filled by the owner for each later admin. The new columns are NOT
-- populated from `auth.users` on read — `full_name` comes from the profile the
-- admin typed, and `email` is copied once at migration time below.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- app_admins — descriptive columns for the owner to fill in
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

alter table public.app_admins
  add column if not exists full_name text not null default '';
alter table public.app_admins
  add column if not exists email text not null default '';

comment on table public.app_admins is
  'Server-internal admin registry. No client policy of any kind; grants and revocations happen only through audited migrations or service-role operations. full_name and email are filled in by hand by the owner for each later admin, so a revoked admin can still be identified after the fact.';

-- The rows already in the table predate these columns, so their email would
-- otherwise render as an empty cell in an audit log. `auth.users` is the
-- authority here and only here: this is a migration, not a client read, and it
-- is written once so a later email change does not rewrite the registry's
-- record of who was an admin and when.
--
-- The guard is narrowed rather than lifted. `reject_admin_update()` refuses
-- *every* UPDATE, which was right when a row held nothing but the grant — but
-- the owner now fills `full_name` and `email` in by hand for each later admin,
-- so an untouched trigger would make those columns unwritable. What must stay
-- immutable is the grant itself, so that is exactly what is refused: any UPDATE
-- that touches `user_id`, `granted_at` or `granted_by`. Revocation remains a
-- DELETE, as the function's own message says.
drop trigger if exists trg_app_admins_no_update on public.app_admins;

create or replace function public.reject_admin_grant_change()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  if new.user_id is distinct from old.user_id
     or new.granted_at is distinct from old.granted_at
     or new.granted_by is distinct from old.granted_by then
    raise exception 'app_admins grant is immutable; revoke by deleting the row'
      using errcode = '55000';
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.reject_admin_grant_change() is
  'Refuses any UPDATE that would change an existing grant (user_id, granted_at, granted_by). The descriptive full_name and email columns stay writable so the owner can fill them in.';

drop trigger if exists trg_app_admins_grant_immutable on public.app_admins;
create trigger trg_app_admins_grant_immutable
  before update on public.app_admins
  for each row
  execute function public.reject_admin_grant_change();

update public.app_admins a
   set email = coalesce(u.email, '')
  from auth.users u
 where u.id = a.user_id
   and a.email = '';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- payment_submissions — who rejected a submission, and when
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- `approved_by` / `approved_at` are the approval record and stay untouched by a
-- rejection. These two are the same idea for the other outcome; without them a
-- rejected payment carries no record of which admin turned it down.
alter table public.payment_submissions
  add column if not exists rejected_by uuid references auth.users (id);
alter table public.payment_submissions
  add column if not exists rejected_at timestamptz;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- is_app_admin() — is the caller an admin?
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- SECURITY DEFINER so the policy and the RPCs below can ask without the client
-- ever holding a grant on the registry itself.
create or replace function public.is_app_admin()
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select public.is_admin();
$$;

comment on function public.is_app_admin() is
  'True when the calling user is a registered admin. The only way a client learns its own admin status — app_admins itself has no client policy, so the row behind this answer is never readable.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- admin_list_payment_submissions() — every submission, with who it is for
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.admin_list_payment_submissions()
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- One row per submission, joined to the payer. The client's RLS policies are
  -- deliberately not consulted: this runs as the owner, which is the point —
  -- it is admin-gated in the function body instead.
  --
  -- `approved_new_expiry` comes from the immutable event rather than from
  -- `profiles.subscription_expiry`, so a Resolved row shows what THIS approval
  -- set, not the account's current expiry several renewals later.
  return (
    select coalesce(
             jsonb_agg(
               jsonb_build_object(
                 'id', s.id,
                 'user_id', s.user_id,
                 'plan_type', s.plan_type,
                 'amount', s.amount,
                 'mfs_provider', s.mfs_provider,
                 'sender_number', s.sender_number,
                 'transaction_id', s.transaction_id,
                 'status', s.status,
                 'admin_notes', s.admin_notes,
                 'submitted_at', s.submitted_at,
                 'approved_at', s.approved_at,
                 'approved_by', s.approved_by,
                 'rejected_at', s.rejected_at,
                 'rejected_by', s.rejected_by,
                 'approved_new_expiry', e.new_expiry,
                 'full_name', p.full_name,
                 'email', p.email,
                 'bar_association', p.bar_association,
                 'membership_no', p.membership_no,
                 'mobile_no', p.mobile_no
               )
               order by s.submitted_at desc
             ),
             '[]'::jsonb
           )
      from public.payment_submissions s
      left join public.profiles p on p.id = s.user_id
      left join public.payment_approval_events e
             on e.payment_submission_id = s.id
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.admin_list_payment_submissions() is
  'Admin-only list of every payment submission joined to the payer profile (name, email, bar association, membership number, mobile). Newest first; the client splits pending from resolved and sorts each.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- admin_reject_payment() — refuse a submission, with a note and a record
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.admin_reject_payment(
  payment_submission_id uuid,
  rejection_note text default null
)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_submission public.payment_submissions%rowtype;
begin
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  select * into v_submission
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.id = payment_submission_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- Idempotency and the same escape hatches as approve_payment: an already
  -- resolved submission is never silently re-resolved, and a rejection can
  -- never undo an approval.
  if v_submission.status <> 'pending' then
    return jsonb_build_object(
      'status', 'already_resolved',
      'current_status', v_submission.status
    );
  end if;

  update public.payment_submissions s
     set status = 'rejected',
         admin_notes = nullif(btrim(rejection_note), ''),
         rejected_by = auth.uid(),
         rejected_at = now(),
         updated_at = (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint
   where s.id = payment_submission_id;

  -- No audit event: `payment_approval_events` records an entitlement change and
  -- requires a non-null new_expiry, which a rejection does not have. The
  -- rejected_by / rejected_at columns above are the record.
  return jsonb_build_object('status', 'rejected');
end;
$$;

comment on function public.admin_reject_payment(uuid, text) is
  'Admin-only rejection of a pending payment submission: sets status, stores the note, and records which admin refused it and when. Returns already_resolved for an approved or previously rejected submission.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Grants
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Supabase's default privileges grant EXECUTE on a new function in `public`
-- directly to `anon` and `authenticated`, and a direct grant outlives a revoke
-- aimed at PUBLIC — so every function here is re-asserted explicitly.
revoke all on function public.is_app_admin() from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.is_app_admin() to authenticated;

revoke all on function public.admin_list_payment_submissions() from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_list_payment_submissions() to authenticated;

revoke all on function public.admin_reject_payment(uuid, text) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_reject_payment(uuid, text) to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.385 — Task 21 Follow-Up — Readable Plan Names On The Payment Ledgers

### 0.0.385 · `20261005120000_payment_plan_name.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-05.** Readable plan names on both payment ledgers: `plan_type` stays the machine key and `payment_submissions` / `payment_approval_events` each gain a generated `plan_name` (`1 Month` / `3 Months` / `6 Months` / `1 Year`), so the raw tables read plainly without touching the approval path or either table's write privileges.

```sql
-- Readable plan names beside the plan_type key, on both payment ledgers.
--
-- `plan_type` on `payment_submissions` and `payment_approval_events` is a
-- machine key (`monthly`, `quarterly`, `half_yearly`, `annual`): `approve_payment`
-- validates it against that exact set and uses a CASE over it to choose both the
-- expected amount and the interval the plan extends by. `plan_name` is its
-- human-readable counterpart — `1 Month`, `3 Months`, `6 Months`, `1 Year` — so
-- the raw tables read plainly in the Supabase table editor and in any SQL output
-- instead of showing a bare key.
--
-- A generated column, not a written one: the value derives from `plan_type` on
-- every read, so it can never drift from the key it names, no data migration is
-- needed (existing rows are computed on the spot) and neither the approval path
-- nor either table's write privileges changes. `plan_type` stays the key.
--
-- The names are English, matching the app's English ARB labels; the app
-- localizes its own display text (Bengali included) from the key. A future plan
-- key this CASE does not yet know shows its raw key rather than a blank.
--
-- Table class: `payment_submissions` and `payment_approval_events` remain
-- server-managed (no client write policy exists for either).

alter table public.payment_submissions
  add column if not exists plan_name text
  generated always as (
    case plan_type
      when 'monthly' then '1 Month'
      when 'quarterly' then '3 Months'
      when 'half_yearly' then '6 Months'
      when 'annual' then '1 Year'
      else plan_type
    end
  ) stored;

comment on column public.payment_submissions.plan_name is
  'Readable name of the plan the submission pays for, derived from plan_type (1 Month / 3 Months / 6 Months / 1 Year). Generated, so it cannot disagree with the key; the app localizes its own labels from plan_type.';

alter table public.payment_approval_events
  add column if not exists plan_name text
  generated always as (
    case plan_type
      when 'monthly' then '1 Month'
      when 'quarterly' then '3 Months'
      when 'half_yearly' then '6 Months'
      when 'annual' then '1 Year'
      else plan_type
    end
  ) stored;

comment on column public.payment_approval_events.plan_name is
  'Readable name of the plan this approval event recorded, derived from plan_type. Generated, so the immutable ledger carries a name that can never disagree with its key.';
```

## App Version 0.0.411 — Task 12 — Deleting The Account Itself

### 0.0.411 · `20261008100000_delete_my_account.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-08.** Task 12's Manage Account actions, built: delete_my_account(), the account holders own complete removal — every domain row, custom reference row, preference, payment submission and approval event, sync receipt, firm link and finally the Auth account — so no residue of it remains. Refuses, changing nothing, for a firm account and for an app administrator, and names the reason.

```sql
-- Task 12's Manage Account actions, built (0.0.411) — delete_my_account():
-- the account holder's own, complete removal of their account.
--
-- Why this exists. Manage Account's **Delete Account** card was the last Part A
-- placeholder on the Settings surface: it warned nothing and did nothing. Making
-- it real cannot be a client query — an account is made of server rows —
-- `profiles`, the domain rows it authored, its preferences, its payment
-- submissions and the `auth.users` row itself — and no client may write any of
-- them (AGENTS.md rule 12). So it is a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, exactly like
-- `abandon_provisional_account()`, with one difference that shapes everything
-- below: that one may only remove an account that provably never did anything,
-- while this one removes an account that has *done* everything, and must leave
-- no residue of it behind.
--
-- Table classes (AGENTS.md rule 4). The function writes across four of them, and
-- gives each the treatment that class requires:
--   * **push/pull domain** (`cases`, `case_history`, `notes`,
--     `case_references`, `conflict_log`, `delete_requests`, and the caller's
--     custom rows in the six editable reference tables) — hard-deleted, because
--     a soft delete is exactly the residue this is for. Nothing is queued and
--     nothing is broadcast: the account is going away, so there is no other
--     device to tell (any device still holding its own copy is wiped on its own
--     next launch, by the sign-out the client runs after this returns).
--   * **pull-only cache** (`profiles`, `firm_members`) — removed with the
--     account.
--   * **per-user preference** (`user_preferences`,
--     `case_notification_preferences`) and **server-managed** (`invite_attempts`,
--     `app_admins`, `mutation_receipts`, `payment_submissions`,
--     `payment_approval_events`) — removed too; row by row where the class allows
--     it, and by `on delete cascade` from `auth.users` where it does not.
--   * **server-managed, and kept**: nothing. "No residue" is the requirement.
--
-- What is refused, and why it must be. Two kinds of account hold rows that are
-- not only theirs, and the client cannot tell them apart from the rest, so the
-- server answers and the screen words the answer:
--   * **a firm account** (`profiles.firm_id`, or owning a `firms` row): the
--     domain rows a firm shares belong to the firm, and deleting them would take
--     a colleague's diary with them. The owner has to remove the account from
--     the firm first (the roster and its remove action are Task 20's), and this
--     function says so instead of guessing.
--   * **an app administrator**: their rows include other people's payment
--     ledger.
-- Both refusals change nothing at all — they are checked before the first
-- `delete`.
--
-- What removal means, in order. Everything that references `auth.users` without
-- `on delete cascade` has to go first, children before parents, because those
-- foreign keys are what would otherwise refuse the final statement:
--   * `delete_requests` requested by the caller; a request they merely
--     **reviewed** is somebody else's row, so only its `reviewed_by` link is
--     cleared (the column is nullable for exactly this kind of case);
--   * `conflict_log`, `case_history` (authored, or about a case of theirs),
--     `notes`, `case_references`;
--   * `case_notification_preferences`, then `cases` — which cascades the
--     remaining history and per-case preferences;
--   * the caller's **custom** reference rows, children before parents: `steps`,
--     `case_types`, `on_behalfs`, `courts`, `case_categories`, then
--     `court_categories`. Seeded rows carry `user_id is null` and are untouched,
--     so the shared baseline every account reads survives;
--   * money: the approval ledger first, whose `payment_submission_id` FK is
--     `on delete restrict` — and which is otherwise immutable, so the trigger is
--     switched off for exactly the two statements that need it (see below) —
--     then the caller's own `payment_submissions`, then their
--     `mutation_receipts` (deleted after the domain rows, since a receipt is the
--     record of a mutation those rows carried);
--   * `firm_invites` the caller issued and `firm_members` rows of theirs (the
--     latter also cascade, but stating it keeps the read honest);
--   * and finally `delete from auth.users`, which cascades `profiles`,
--     `user_preferences`, `case_notification_preferences`, `invite_attempts`,
```
