```sql
--     `app_admins` and `firm_members` — and with them the account's sessions and
--     refresh tokens, so nothing can be resumed afterwards.
--
-- The ledger's trigger, switched off on purpose. `payment_approval_events` is an
-- immutable audit ledger: `trg_payment_approval_events_immutable` raises on any
-- UPDATE or DELETE, for every role ("for every role that does not first disable
-- the trigger deliberately" — its own comment). Removing an account means the
-- ledger rows naming it have to go: they are `not null` references, they cannot
-- be anonymized in place, and its `payment_submission_id` FK is
-- `on delete restrict`, so the submission could not go either. The two
-- statements below are the deliberate exception the trigger's comment allows.
-- They run inside this transaction with their `delete`, so the trigger can never
-- be left disabled — a failure rolls the whole thing back, trigger included.
--
-- Returns the project's usual envelope: `{"status":"removed"}`,
-- `{"status":"refused","reason":"firm"|"administrator"}`, or
-- `{"status":"unauthenticated"}`.

create or replace function public.delete_my_account()
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_user_id uuid := auth.uid();
  v_firm_id uuid;
  v_refusal text;
begin
  if v_user_id is null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'unauthenticated');
  end if;

  -- Refusal 1: a firm account. Checked against both halves of "belongs to a
  -- firm" — the profile's own firm link (what the app calls firm mode) and
  -- ownership of a firm row, which is what the domain rows are shared through.
  select p.firm_id into v_firm_id from public.profiles p where p.id = v_user_id;

  if v_firm_id is not null
     or exists (select 1 from public.firms f where f.owner_id = v_user_id) then
    v_refusal := 'firm';
  end if;

  -- Refusal 2: an administrator. `app_admins` is server-internal, and an admin's
  -- rows include the payment ledger of every other account.
  if v_refusal is null
     and exists (select 1 from public.app_admins a where a.user_id = v_user_id)
  then
    v_refusal := 'administrator';
  end if;

  if v_refusal is not null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', v_refusal);
  end if;

  -- --- Domain rows: everything the caller authored, children before parents. ---

  -- The caller's own requests go. A request they reviewed belongs to somebody
  -- else, so the reviewer link is the only part of it that is theirs.
  update public.delete_requests
     set reviewed_by = null
   where reviewed_by = v_user_id;
  delete from public.delete_requests where requested_by = v_user_id;

  delete from public.conflict_log where user_id = v_user_id;

  -- Authored history, plus any history of a case of theirs (the rest cascades
  -- from `cases` below).
  delete from public.case_history
   where user_id = v_user_id or created_by = v_user_id;

  delete from public.notes where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.case_references where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.case_notification_preferences where user_id = v_user_id;

  -- Cases last among the case data: `court_id`, `case_category_id` and
  -- `on_behalf_id` point at the reference rows deleted below.
  delete from public.cases where user_id = v_user_id;

  -- Custom reference rows. Seeded rows have `user_id is null`, so the baseline
  -- every account reads is untouched. Steps, case types and on-behalfs point at
  -- a court category, case types may point at a case category, and courts point
  -- at a court category: children first, `court_categories` last.
  delete from public.steps where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.case_types where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.on_behalfs where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.courts where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.case_categories where user_id = v_user_id;
  delete from public.court_categories where user_id = v_user_id;

  -- --- Money and audit rows. ---

  -- The deliberate exception described in the header: the ledger refuses deletes
  -- for every role, and this is the one legitimate reason to switch it off. Both
  -- statements and the delete are in this transaction, so the trigger is never
  -- left disabled.
  alter table public.payment_approval_events
    disable trigger trg_payment_approval_events_immutable;
  delete from public.payment_approval_events
   where user_id = v_user_id or approved_by = v_user_id;
  alter table public.payment_approval_events
    enable trigger trg_payment_approval_events_immutable;

  delete from public.payment_submissions where user_id = v_user_id;

  -- After the domain rows: a receipt is the record of a mutation they carried.
  delete from public.mutation_receipts where actor_id = v_user_id;

  -- --- Firm links the callers of a refused account cannot have; stated anyway. ---

  delete from public.firm_invites where created_by = v_user_id;
  delete from public.firm_members where user_id = v_user_id;

  -- --- The account itself. ---
  --
  -- One statement, and it cannot fail on a foreign key: every reference that does
  -- not cascade has been removed above, and the ones that do — `profiles`,
  -- `user_preferences`, `case_notification_preferences`, `invite_attempts`,
  -- `app_admins`, `firm_members` — go with it, along with the Auth sessions.
  delete from auth.users u where u.id = v_user_id;

  return jsonb_build_object('status', 'removed');
end;
$$;

comment on function public.delete_my_account() is
  'The account holder''s own complete deletion: removes every row of the caller''s account — domain rows, preferences, payment submissions and their approval events, sync receipts, firm links, and the Auth account itself — leaving no residue. Refuses, changing nothing, for a firm account (profiles.firm_id or firms.owner_id) and for an app administrator, returning {"status":"refused","reason":…}; otherwise {"status":"removed"}. Takes no arguments: it can only ever act on auth.uid().';

-- A direct EXECUTE grant from Supabase's default privileges outlives a revoke of
-- PUBLIC, so `anon` is named explicitly — the same reason join_firm,
-- approve_payment, abandon_provisional_account and ensure_firm do it.
revoke all on function public.delete_my_account() from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.delete_my_account() to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.413 — Task 17 — Correcting A Mistyped Sign-Up Address

### 0.0.413 · `20261008110000_discard_unconfirmed_signup.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-08.** The Check Your Email screen, extended: discard_unconfirmed_signup(), the password-checked removal of a sign-up that never confirmed its address — the only way to correct a mistyped one, since an unconfirmed account cannot sign in and abandon_provisional_account() needs a session it can never have. Rate-limited per address in the new server-managed signup_discard_attempts ledger, and granted to anon because this caller has no session at all.

```sql
-- discard_unconfirmed_signup(): the password-checked cleanup behind the Check
-- Your Email screen's **Change Email Address**.
--
-- Why this exists. A user who mistypes their address at sign-up is stuck: the
-- account exists, and Supabase refuses every call that could correct it, because
-- an unconfirmed address can never sign in. Nothing on the device can help
-- either — the address is the account, and it lives only on the server. The
-- app's existing cleanup, `abandon_provisional_account()`, cannot be used here:
-- it works from `auth.uid()`, and this caller has no session by definition.
--
-- What it does, in this order:
--   1. normalizes the address and finds the account by it, case-insensitively;
--   2. verifies the password against that account's own hash (`crypt()` from
--      pgcrypto, which reads the bcrypt `$2a$` hashes this project stores). This
--      is the only proof that the caller is the person who made the account —
--      which is the whole reason a stranger cannot use this to delete other
--      people's pending sign-ups;
--   3. refuses an address that is **already confirmed** (log in instead);
--   4. refuses an account that has done anything, with exactly the guards
--      `abandon_provisional_account()` uses — a firm, a role, a subscription,
--      firm ownership or membership, an issued invite, any authored row, a
--      payment, an approval, administrator rights, a sync receipt;
--   5. removes the `auth.users` row, which cascades the profile, the preference
--      rows, the invite attempts and the sessions.
--
-- The caller may be anonymous — there is no session to have — so it is granted
-- to `anon` as well as `authenticated`, and that is why it is **rate-limited**:
-- five failures per address inside fifteen minutes, recorded in
-- `public.signup_discard_attempts`, and a success clears that address's rows.
-- Nothing in the answer tells a stranger whether an address exists: a missing
-- account, a wrong password and a hash this server cannot read all answer the
-- same `refused`, and only a caller who holds the password learns anything more.
--
-- Table classification (AGENTS.md rule 4): `signup_discard_attempts` is
-- **server-managed** — a rolling rate-limit ledger, exactly like
-- `invite_attempts`. It is never synced, never pulled and never written by a
-- client; RLS is on with no policies, so only this function (which is SECURITY
-- DEFINER) can touch it.
--
-- ponytail: the ledger is keyed by address rather than by account, because the
-- caller has no account id yet, and only this function's own success and its
-- one-day retention purge clear rows. The ceiling is that removing an account
-- through `delete_my_account()` leaves that address's attempt rows behind for up
-- to a day; the upgrade path, if it ever matters, is one more delete line in
-- that function.

create table if not exists public.signup_discard_attempts (
  email text not null,
  attempted_at timestamptz not null default now()
);

comment on table public.signup_discard_attempts is
  'Rolling rate-limit ledger for discard_unconfirmed_signup(): one row per refused proof, keyed by the address it was refused for. Server-managed; no client access.';

create index if not exists signup_discard_attempts_email_attempted_at_idx
  on public.signup_discard_attempts (email, attempted_at desc);

alter table public.signup_discard_attempts enable row level security;

create or replace function public.discard_unconfirmed_signup(
  p_email text,
  p_password text
)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public, extensions
as $$
declare
  -- Five failures per address per quarter hour, and attempts older than a day
  -- are dropped by the purge below — the same shape join_firm() uses.
  c_window constant interval := interval '15 minutes';
  c_max_attempts constant int := 5;
  c_retention constant interval := interval '1 day';

  v_email text := lower(btrim(coalesce(p_email, '')));
  v_attempts int;
  v_user auth.users%rowtype;
  v_reason text;
begin
  -- Nothing to look up without both halves of the proof.
  if v_email = '' or coalesce(p_password, '') = '' then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'not_provisional');
  end if;

  delete from public.signup_discard_attempts a
   where a.attempted_at < now() - c_retention;

  select count(*)
    into v_attempts
    from public.signup_discard_attempts a
   where a.email = v_email
     and a.attempted_at > now() - c_window;

  if v_attempts >= c_max_attempts then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'rate_limited');
  end if;

  select u.* into v_user from auth.users u where lower(u.email) = v_email;

  -- One answer for every way the proof can fail: no such account, no hash, a
  -- wrong password, or a hash this server cannot read (crypt() answers null
  -- then, and `is distinct from` is what keeps that a refusal rather than a
  -- match). The attempt is recorded through a normal `return`, never by raising
  -- after an insert — a raise would roll the attempt back with the statement and
  -- the limit would never fill up.
  if not found
     or v_user.encrypted_password is null
     or crypt(p_password, v_user.encrypted_password)
        is distinct from v_user.encrypted_password
  then
    insert into public.signup_discard_attempts (email) values (v_email);
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'not_provisional');
  end if;

  -- A confirmed address is not a waiting sign-up: the account is real, and the
  -- user's way forward is the login they can now pass.
  if v_user.email_confirmed_at is not null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'confirmed');
  end if;

  -- The account's profile. A missing row is a bug state, not a provisional
  -- account, so nothing is removed.
  select case
           when p.firm_id is not null then 'a firm'
           when p.role <> 'individual' then 'a role'
           when p.subscription_expiry is not null then 'a subscription'
         end
    into v_reason
    from public.profiles p
   where p.id = v_user.id;

  if not found then
    v_reason := 'no profile';
  end if;

  -- Everything the account may have done, exactly as abandon_provisional_account
  -- lists it: the first table with a row refuses the removal. Each statement is
  -- either a row whose loss would be a loss of work, or a reference that does not
  -- cascade from auth.users — which is why the removal below cannot fail on a
  -- foreign key.
  if v_reason is null then
    select t.reason
      into v_reason
      from (
        select 'firm ownership' as reason
          from public.firms f where f.owner_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'firm membership'
          from public.firm_members m where m.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'an issued invite'
          from public.firm_invites i where i.created_by = v_user.id
        union all select 'a payment'
          from public.payment_submissions s where s.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'an approval'
          from public.payment_approval_events e
         where e.user_id = v_user.id or e.approved_by = v_user.id
        union all select 'administrator rights'
          from public.app_admins a where a.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'a sync receipt'
          from public.mutation_receipts r where r.actor_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'cases'
          from public.cases c where c.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'case history'
          from public.case_history h where h.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'notes'
          from public.notes n where n.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'case references'
          from public.case_references r where r.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'delete requests'
          from public.delete_requests d
         where d.requested_by = v_user.id or d.reviewed_by = v_user.id
        union all select 'conflict records'
          from public.conflict_log l where l.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'custom courts'
          from public.courts x where x.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'custom court categories'
          from public.court_categories x where x.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'custom case categories'
          from public.case_categories x where x.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'custom steps'
          from public.steps x where x.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'custom case types'
          from public.case_types x where x.user_id = v_user.id
        union all select 'custom on-behalfs'
          from public.on_behalfs x where x.user_id = v_user.id
      ) t
     limit 1;
  end if;

  if v_reason is not null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'not_provisional');
  end if;

  -- Provisional, unconfirmed, and the password proved the caller: remove the
  -- account, and forget the attempts it took to get here.
  delete from public.signup_discard_attempts a where a.email = v_email;
  delete from auth.users u where u.id = v_user.id;

  return jsonb_build_object('status', 'removed');
end;
$$;

comment on function public.discard_unconfirmed_signup(text, text) is
  'Removes a sign-up that has never confirmed its address, when the caller proves ownership with the account password: the only way to correct a mistyped address, because an unconfirmed account cannot sign in and abandon_provisional_account() needs a session it can never have. Rate-limited per address. Returns {"status":"removed"}, {"status":"refused","reason":"confirmed"|"not_provisional"} or {"status":"rate_limited"}.';

-- `anon` must be named explicitly: a direct EXECUTE grant from Supabase's default
-- privileges outlives a revoke of PUBLIC, and this caller has no session — which
-- is the same reason join_firm and abandon_provisional_account name their roles.
revoke all on function public.discard_unconfirmed_signup(text, text)
  from public;
grant execute on function public.discard_unconfirmed_signup(text, text)
  to anon, authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.459 — Task 23 Follow-Up — The Admin Panel's Non-Verified Accounts

### 0.0.459 · `20261009120000_admin_unconfirmed_accounts.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-09.** The Admin Panel, extended: the Non-verified Accounts page's two admin-gated RPCs. admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() lists every account that has never confirmed its address — invisible to clients, since email_confirmed_at lives in auth.users and profiles carries no confirmation flag — and admin_delete_unconfirmed_account() removes one. Both share is_provisional_account(), the same "has this account done anything?" guard discard_unconfirmed_signup() applies, so the list's can_delete flag and the delete can never disagree.

```sql
-- admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() / admin_delete_unconfirmed_account():
-- the Admin Panel's **Non-verified Accounts** page.
--
-- Why this exists. A sign-up that never confirms its address is invisible to
-- every client: `email_confirmed_at` lives in `auth.users`, which no client may
-- read, and the `profiles` row carries no confirmation flag (the sign-up trigger
-- creates it before any confirmation, so an unconfirmed account has an ordinary
-- profile row). Abandoned sign-ups therefore accumulate where nobody can see
-- them. These two functions are the administrator's window on them: one lists
-- them, one removes one.
--
-- The test for "may be removed" is the **same** guard `discard_unconfirmed_signup()`
-- uses — unconfirmed, and having done nothing at all: no firm, no membership, no
-- invite, no payment or approval, no administrator rights, no sync receipt and no
-- authored row of any kind. It is factored into `is_provisional_account()` so the
-- list and the delete can never disagree about which accounts qualify; the list
-- reports it as `can_delete`, so the panel disables the action rather than
-- letting an administrator press it and be refused.
--
-- Nothing here is reachable by a non-administrator: both functions raise
-- `admin_required` unless `is_admin()` answers true, and both are SECURITY
-- DEFINER reading `auth.users` as the owner — which is the point, since no client
-- policy grants access to that table.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- is_provisional_account() — may this account still be removed?
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- True when the account has done nothing that its removal would destroy. The
-- tables below are exactly `discard_unconfirmed_signup()`'s list: each is either
-- a row whose loss would be a loss of work, or a reference that does not cascade
-- from `auth.users` — which is also why the deletion below cannot fail on a
-- foreign key.
--
-- SECURITY DEFINER so it may read those tables from inside the admin functions
-- whichever role is calling; it is not granted to any client role.
create or replace function public.is_provisional_account(p_user_id uuid)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select not exists (
    select 1
      from (
        select 1 from public.firms f where f.owner_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.firm_members m where m.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.firm_invites i where i.created_by = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.payment_submissions s where s.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.payment_approval_events e
         where e.user_id = p_user_id or e.approved_by = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.app_admins a where a.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.mutation_receipts r where r.actor_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.cases c where c.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.case_history h where h.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.notes n where n.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.case_references r where r.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.delete_requests d
         where d.requested_by = p_user_id or d.reviewed_by = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.conflict_log l where l.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.courts x where x.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.court_categories x where x.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.case_categories x where x.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.steps x where x.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.case_types x where x.user_id = p_user_id
        union all select 1 from public.on_behalfs x where x.user_id = p_user_id
      ) activity
  );
$$;

comment on function public.is_provisional_account(uuid) is
  'True when the account has done nothing (no firm, membership, invite, payment, approval, admin rights, sync receipt or authored row). The shared test behind admin_delete_unconfirmed_account() and the panel''s can_delete flag; the same guard discard_unconfirmed_signup() applies.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() — the sign-ups that never confirmed
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Newest first. `email` prefers the profile's copy (which mirrors the account
  -- address) and falls back to `auth.users` for a profile row that has not been
  -- written yet; `full_name` is empty until the account fills it in.
  return (
    select coalesce(
             jsonb_agg(
               jsonb_build_object(
                 'id', u.id,
                 'email', coalesce(nullif(p.email, ''), u.email),
                 'full_name', coalesce(p.full_name, ''),
                 'created_at', u.created_at,
                 'last_sign_in_at', u.last_sign_in_at,
                 'can_delete', public.is_provisional_account(u.id)
               )
               order by u.created_at desc
             ),
             '[]'::jsonb
           )
      from auth.users u
      left join public.profiles p on p.id = u.id
     where u.email_confirmed_at is null
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() is
  'Admin-only list of every account that has never confirmed its address, joined to its profile for the name and the mirrored email. Newest first; each row carries can_delete, the shared provisional test, so the panel can disable an action that would be refused.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- admin_delete_unconfirmed_account() — remove one abandoned sign-up
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(
  p_user_id uuid
)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_user auth.users%rowtype;
begin
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  select u.* into v_user from auth.users u where u.id = p_user_id;
  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- A confirmed address is a real account, however empty: the administrator's
  -- way to deal with it is not this function.
  if v_user.email_confirmed_at is not null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'confirmed');
  end if;

  -- Re-run the guard even though the list reported it: the account may have
  -- done something between the two calls.
  if not public.is_provisional_account(p_user_id) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'has_activity');
  end if;

  -- Removing the account cascades the profile, the preference rows, the invite
  -- attempts and the sessions — nothing else references it, which the guard
  -- above has just established.
  delete from auth.users u where u.id = p_user_id;

  return jsonb_build_object('status', 'removed');
end;
$$;

comment on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid) is
  'Admin-only removal of an account that has never confirmed its address and has done nothing. Refuses an unknown, confirmed or non-provisional account. Returns {"status":"removed"} or {"status":"refused","reason":"not_found"|"confirmed"|"has_activity"}.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Grants
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Supabase's default privileges grant EXECUTE on a new function in `public`
-- directly to `anon` and `authenticated`, and a direct grant outlives a revoke
-- aimed at PUBLIC — so every function here is re-asserted explicitly. The shared
-- guard is granted to no client role: only the SECURITY DEFINER functions above
-- call it, as the owner.
revoke all on function public.is_provisional_account(uuid) from public, anon;

revoke all on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
  from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
  to authenticated;

revoke all on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)
  from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)
  to authenticated;
```

### 0.0.459 · `20261009130000_revoke_provisional_guard.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-09.** Closing a grant the default privileges opened: taking EXECUTE on is_provisional_account() back from `authenticated`. Revoking `public` and `anon` does not cover it, because Supabase grants a new function in `public` directly to `authenticated` as well — so the internal guard was callable by any signed-in account, one boolean at a time about an account it already knew the id of.

```sql
-- is_provisional_account() is internal: take back the EXECUTE that Supabase's
-- default privileges handed to `authenticated`.
--
-- Why this exists. The migration that added the Admin Panel's
-- Non-verified Accounts RPCs (`20261009120000`) intends the shared guard to be
-- reachable by **no client role** — only the two SECURITY DEFINER functions
-- above it call it, as the owner. It revoked `public` and `anon`, which is the
-- shape the rest of this project's functions use, and did not grant
-- `authenticated` any more than it granted `anon`.
--
-- That is not enough for a function that is *not* meant to be a client entry
-- point. Supabase's default privileges grant EXECUTE on a new function in
-- `public` **directly** to each client role — including `authenticated` — and a
-- direct grant outlives a revoke aimed at PUBLIC, the note the admin panel's own
-- migration carries. So `authenticated` kept a grant this function was never
-- supposed to have: any signed-in account could call
-- `is_provisional_account(<any uuid>)` and learn, one boolean at a time,
-- whether another account had done anything at all. SECURITY DEFINER means it
-- answers by reading tables that caller cannot read.
--
-- The exposure is a boolean about an account whose id the caller must already
-- know, not the data behind it, but the guard is internal by design and this
-- closes it. The server's own bypass role keeps its grant, as it does on every
-- function in this project, and is deliberately not named here — the ledger
-- test forbids that string in committed SQL.

revoke execute on function public.is_provisional_account(uuid)
  from authenticated;

-- Re-asserted so the file states the whole grant picture in one place: the two
-- admin entry points stay callable by a signed-in account (the function body
-- gates them), and the guard stays internal.
revoke all on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
  from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
  to authenticated;

revoke all on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)
  from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)
  to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.495 — Task 23 Follow-Up — Non-Verified Accounts Waits 72 Hours

### 0.0.495 · `20261012100000_unconfirmed_accounts_72h_window.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-12.** The Non-verified Accounts page waits 72 hours before it calls a sign-up abandoned: admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() returns only an account created over 72 hours ago, and admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid) refuses a younger one as too_recent before its activity test, so the window is enforced and not merely hidden. is_provisional_account() is untouched — age and activity are different questions — and the grants stay as the previous migration left them.

```sql
-- The Non-verified Accounts page shows only sign-ups that have sat unconfirmed
-- for **72 hours**, and its delete refuses anything younger.
--
-- Why this exists. `20261009120000` listed every account whose
-- `email_confirmed_at` is still null — and therefore listed a sign-up the moment
-- it was created. A user who has just registered is normally mid-verification,
-- not abandoned: they are walking to their mailbox, and a slow mail server or a
-- closed app is not a reason to show them to an administrator as a stray. A
-- window gives a genuine sign-up its chance to confirm, so the page means
-- "abandoned" rather than "started", and the side-menu badge (which counts this
-- list) stops flicking up by one every time somebody taps Sign Up.
--
-- What changes. Only the horizon, and only here:
--   * `admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()` gains
--     `and u.created_at < now() - c_min_age`, so a fresh sign-up is not returned;
--   * `admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)` re-runs that same window before
--     the provisional test and refuses a too-young account as `too_recent`,
--     **not** as `has_activity`. Without this the window would be cosmetic: a
--     client that knew the id could still remove a sign-up the page refuses to
--     show, and `has_activity` would be a false reason for an account that has
--     done nothing at all.
--
-- The window lives in one constant per function (`c_min_age`) rather than in a
-- setting, because it is a policy of this page, not a preference: the list, the
-- badge and the delete must agree on it, and they all read it from this file's
-- definition of the two functions. `is_provisional_account()` is untouched — the
-- window is about *age*, the guard is about *activity*, and they answer
-- different questions.
--
-- Everything else is the predecessor's behaviour re-asserted unchanged: both
-- functions stay SECURITY DEFINER and admin-gated, the list stays newest-first
-- with `can_delete` per row, and the grants stay exactly as `20261009130000`
-- left them (the two entry points callable by `authenticated` and gated in the
-- body, with the internal guard reachable by no client role).

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() — the oldest never-confirmed sign-ups
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- How long an unconfirmed sign-up must have sat before the page calls it
  -- abandoned. A sign-up younger than this may still be confirming.
  c_min_age constant interval := interval '72 hours';
begin
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Newest first. `email` prefers the profile's copy (which mirrors the account
  -- address) and falls back to `auth.users` for a profile row that has not been
  -- written yet; `full_name` is empty until the account fills it in. The age
  -- window is the only addition since `20261009120000`.
  return (
    select coalesce(
             jsonb_agg(
               jsonb_build_object(
                 'id', u.id,
                 'email', coalesce(nullif(p.email, ''), u.email),
                 'full_name', coalesce(p.full_name, ''),
                 'created_at', u.created_at,
                 'last_sign_in_at', u.last_sign_in_at,
                 'can_delete', public.is_provisional_account(u.id)
               )
               order by u.created_at desc
             ),
             '[]'::jsonb
           )
      from auth.users u
      left join public.profiles p on p.id = u.id
     where u.email_confirmed_at is null
       and u.created_at < now() - c_min_age
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts() is
  'Admin-only list of every account that has never confirmed its address AND was created more than 72 hours ago, joined to its profile for the name and the mirrored email. Newest first; each row carries can_delete, the shared provisional test, so the panel can disable an action that would be refused. A sign-up younger than the window is deliberately absent: it may still be confirming.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- admin_delete_unconfirmed_account() — remove one abandoned sign-up
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(
  p_user_id uuid
)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- The same window the list applies. Kept equal to it by inspection, the way
  -- the two already share `is_provisional_account()` for the activity test.
  c_min_age constant interval := interval '72 hours';
  v_user auth.users%rowtype;
begin
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  select u.* into v_user from auth.users u where u.id = p_user_id;
  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- A confirmed address is a real account, however empty: the administrator's
  -- way to deal with it is not this function.
  if v_user.email_confirmed_at is not null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'confirmed');
  end if;

  -- Too young to call abandoned, so not this function's to remove yet — the
  -- same horizon the list hides it behind. Refused *before* the activity test,
  -- and with its own reason, so a fresh sign-up is never reported as an account
  -- that has done something when it has done nothing.
  if v_user.created_at >= now() - c_min_age then
    return jsonb_build_object(
      'status', 'refused', 'reason', 'too_recent'
    );
  end if;

  -- Re-run the guard even though the list reported it: the account may have
  -- done something between the two calls.
  if not public.is_provisional_account(p_user_id) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'refused', 'reason', 'has_activity');
  end if;

  -- Removing the account cascades the profile, the preference rows, the invite
  -- attempts and the sessions — nothing else references it, which the guards
  -- above have just established.
  delete from auth.users u where u.id = p_user_id;

  return jsonb_build_object('status', 'removed');
end;
$$;

comment on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid) is
  'Admin-only removal of an account that has never confirmed its address, was created more than 72 hours ago, and has done nothing. Refuses an unknown, confirmed, too-recent or non-provisional account. Returns {"status":"removed"} or {"status":"refused","reason":"not_found"|"confirmed"|"too_recent"|"has_activity"}.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Grants — re-asserted so this file states the whole grant picture in one place
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Supabase's default privileges grant EXECUTE on a new function in `public`
-- directly to `anon` and `authenticated`, and a direct grant outlives a revoke
-- aimed at PUBLIC — so every function here is re-asserted explicitly, exactly as
-- `20261009130000` left it. The internal guard is named only to keep it closed:
-- it is granted to no client role.
revoke all on function public.is_provisional_account(uuid)
  from public, anon, authenticated;

revoke all on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
  from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_list_unconfirmed_accounts()
  to authenticated;

revoke all on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)
  from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.admin_delete_unconfirmed_account(uuid)
  to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.498 — Admin Payment Alerts Phase 2 — The Push Server Half

### 0.0.498 · `20261012120000_admin_payment_alerts.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-12.** The server half of the admin push notification: device_tokens (one row per device, self-only under RLS, deleted by its own device on the way out of an account), the per-user payment_alerts_enabled toggle on user_preferences (default on), the two reads the sender runs — the pending-payment count and the fan-set — revoked from every client role, and pg_net plus two triggers on payment_submissions that post the change to the send-admin-payment-alerts Edge Function with the Vault-held shared secret.

```sql
-- Admin payment alerts — the server half of the admin push notification.
--
-- Why this exists. An administrator must learn that a payment request is waiting,
-- and the only shape that is a real notification is one that arrives while the app
-- is closed — which needs a push sender. The sender itself is an Edge Function and
-- is therefore not in this file; what is here is everything it stands on:
--
--   * `device_tokens` — where a signed-in device registers the FCM token it was
--     given. Classified **local operational**: per-device state the client writes
--     about itself, never firm-shared, never part of the delta pull, read only by
--     the sender.
--   * `user_preferences.payment_alerts_enabled` — the per-user toggle from
--     Settings → Notifications, default **on** (an admin who installs the panel
--     wants to hear about payments waiting, and can silence it). The table stays
--     in the per-user preference class: self-only, never firm-shared.
--   * `push_pending_payment_count()` and `push_alert_targets()` — the two reads the
--     sender needs, left to the server's own API role **alone**. Further down, why
--     they are not `security definer` and why the token list must have no client
--     path.
--
-- Whose role that is, said once so the rest of the file can stay quiet about it:
-- the server's role is the one PostgREST uses with the secret key, and Supabase's
-- default privileges grant every new function **and** every new table in `public`
-- to it directly — exactly as they do for `anon` and `authenticated`. That is why
-- the revokes below name only the client roles: naming them is what closes a door,
-- and the server's door is the same default grant the whole schema already relies
-- on. (No migration in this repository names that role in SQL, and the structural
-- contract test fails if one does — a shipped grant is not a credential.)
--   * `pg_net` and one trigger on `payment_submissions`, which posts to the sender
--     whenever a submission appears or its status changes.
--
-- One value is deliberately absent, and is the only manual step: the shared secret
-- the trigger sends and the function compares. It lives in Supabase Vault as
-- `fcm_webhook_secret`, and the same value is set on the function as
-- `FCM_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. Missing, the trigger raises a warning and sends nothing —
-- a missing notification must never be the reason a payment fails.
--
-- What is deliberately not stored: no count, no "last notified at". The sender
-- recomputes the pending count on every change, so the number a notification shows
-- is the number the server holds at that moment and a missed push leaves nothing
-- stale behind.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- device_tokens — one row per device that wants push, written by that device
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.device_tokens (
  -- The FCM registration token *is* the identity: it is unique per app install,
  -- and re-registering must update the row rather than add a second one. As the
  -- primary key it also settles what a shared device does — registering the same
  -- token under another account reassigns the row (`on conflict (token) do
  -- update set user_id = ...`), so a phone that changes hands stops feeding the
  -- account that left, which is the same rule Task 23's 0.0.394 leak fix applied
  -- to the panel itself.
  token text primary key,
  -- Deleting the account — Task 12's delete_my_account, or an administrator
  -- removing an abandoned sign-up — takes its tokens with it.
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  -- Server-owned, because a device's clock is not evidence of anything. Refreshed
  -- by the trigger below on every re-registration, so a stale row is visible when
  -- a device stops coming back.
  last_seen_at timestamptz not null default now()
);

comment on table public.device_tokens is
  'Per-device FCM registration tokens: written by the device itself, read only by the admin payment-alert sender through an RPC no client role can execute. A row follows the account, so a device that changes hands stops feeding the previous one.';

-- The fan-set joins on user_id; the primary key already covers the token lookup.
create index if not exists device_tokens_user_id_idx
  on public.device_tokens (user_id);

create or replace function public.touch_device_token()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  new.last_seen_at := now();
  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.touch_device_token() is
  'Keeps device_tokens.last_seen_at server-timed on every re-registration; a device cannot claim when it was last seen.';

drop trigger if exists trg_device_tokens_touch on public.device_tokens;
create trigger trg_device_tokens_touch
before update on public.device_tokens
for each row execute function public.touch_device_token();

-- Self-only, like every other per-account row. The policies are written with the
-- wrapped `(select auth.uid())` the performance advisor asks for — the documented
-- fix for the existing policies, applied here from the start.
alter table public.device_tokens enable row level security;

drop policy if exists device_tokens_select_own on public.device_tokens;
create policy device_tokens_select_own on public.device_tokens
  for select to authenticated
  using (user_id = (select auth.uid()));

drop policy if exists device_tokens_insert_own on public.device_tokens;
create policy device_tokens_insert_own on public.device_tokens
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (user_id = (select auth.uid()));

drop policy if exists device_tokens_update_own on public.device_tokens;
create policy device_tokens_update_own on public.device_tokens
  for update to authenticated
  using (user_id = (select auth.uid()))
  with check (user_id = (select auth.uid()));

-- DELETE matters as much as the rest: an admin who logs out must stop receiving
-- alerts on that device, so the client removes its own row on the way out.
drop policy if exists device_tokens_delete_own on public.device_tokens;
create policy device_tokens_delete_own on public.device_tokens
  for delete to authenticated
  using (user_id = (select auth.uid()));

-- Supabase's default privileges grant every new table to `anon`, and a direct
-- grant outlives a revoke aimed at PUBLIC — the lesson of 20260929102500 — so
-- `anon` is named. The four verbs a device needs on its own row are granted back;
-- RLS is what narrows them to that row.
revoke all on table public.device_tokens from public, anon;
grant select, insert, update, delete on table public.device_tokens
  to authenticated;
-- The server's own role needs nothing stated: the default grant above covers it,
-- and the sender reads the fan-set through an RPC and prunes a dead token as that
-- role.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- The per-user toggle — Settings → Notifications
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

alter table public.user_preferences
  add column if not exists payment_alerts_enabled boolean not null default true;

comment on column public.user_preferences.payment_alerts_enabled is
  'Whether this account wants a push when a payment request is waiting (Settings → Notifications). Defaults to true. Self-only and writable by its owner through the grants the table already carries, so the toggle needs no privilege of its own; never firm-shared (Rev 2.4 scope contract).';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- The two reads the sender needs — the server's role only
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Both are plain invoker functions on purpose: the only role left able to execute
-- them already bypasses RLS, so a `security definer` wrapper would add escalation
-- surface and buy nothing.

create or replace function public.push_pending_payment_count()
returns integer
language sql
stable
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  -- Payments only, by the design decision taken with the user: the unverified
  -- accounts half cannot be event-driven (nothing writes when a sign-up crosses
  -- the 72-hour line), so it is not part of this number.
  select count(*)::integer
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.status = 'pending';
$$;

comment on function public.push_pending_payment_count() is
  'How many payment submissions are pending right now — the number a notification carries. Reachable by the server''s own API role only: every client role is revoked.';

create or replace function public.push_alert_targets()
returns table (user_id uuid, token text)
language sql
stable
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  -- Administrators with a registered device, minus anyone who turned the toggle
  -- off. A missing preference row means "never touched it", which is the column's
  -- own default of true — hence the coalesce rather than an inner join. ponytail:
  -- an admin whose toggle is off is skipped entirely, including the count-reached-
  -- zero correction; their device clears the stale line when the app is next
  -- opened, and honouring the toggle at all is worth more than that edge.
  select t.user_id, t.token
    from public.device_tokens t
    join public.app_admins a on a.user_id = t.user_id
    left join public.user_preferences p on p.user_id = t.user_id
   where coalesce(p.payment_alerts_enabled, true)
   order by t.created_at;
$$;

comment on function public.push_alert_targets() is
  'Every device belonging to an administrator who has not switched payment alerts off — the fan-set the FCM sender posts to. Reachable by the server''s own API role only: the token list has no client-facing path.';

-- All three are revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated` explicitly: a
-- direct grant to `anon`/`authenticated` (which Supabase's default privileges
-- create) outlives a revoke aimed at PUBLIC, the lesson of 20261009130000. Nothing
-- is granted back, because the server's own role already holds its default grant
-- and no client role may execute any of them.
revoke all on function public.touch_device_token() from public, anon, authenticated;
revoke all on function public.push_pending_payment_count()
  from public, anon, authenticated;
revoke all on function public.push_alert_targets() from public, anon, authenticated;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- The webhook — pg_net posts to the sender when a submission appears or changes
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Installed into `extensions`, not `public`: pg_net's own objects still land in
-- the `net` schema, so `net.http_post` is exactly the same call, and the security
-- advisor's "extension in public" warning stays away. pg_net is not relocatable
-- (`alter extension ... set schema` is refused: "does not support SET SCHEMA"), so
-- an install already sitting in `public` is dropped and made again. The drop is
-- guarded, and it is safe here because nothing used pg_net before this migration —
-- this project had no webhook at all — so there is no queue to lose.
do $$
begin
  if exists (
    select 1 from pg_extension e
     where e.extname = 'pg_net'
       and e.extnamespace <> 'extensions'::regnamespace
  ) then
    drop extension pg_net;
  end if;
end;
$$;

create extension if not exists pg_net with schema extensions;

create or replace function public.notify_admin_payment_alerts()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- The allow-listed project (AGENTS.md rule 12) and the deployed function's
  -- slug. Both are public facts — the same host the app already calls — so
  -- neither is a secret; the secret is the Vault value read below.
  c_url constant text :=
    'https://lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau.supabase.co/functions/v1/send-admin-payment-alerts';
  v_secret text;
begin
  select s.decrypted_secret
    into v_secret
    from vault.decrypted_secrets s
   where s.name = 'fcm_webhook_secret';

  if v_secret is null then
    -- Loud, but never fatal: the submission is already saved and a missing
    -- notification is not a reason to fail a payment. This is what an unarmed
    -- hook looks like in the logs.
    raise warning
      'admin payment alerts: vault secret fcm_webhook_secret is missing; nothing sent';
    return null;
  end if;

  perform net.http_post(
    url := c_url,
    body := jsonb_build_object(
      'type', tg_op,
      'table', 'payment_submissions',
      'schema', 'public',
      'record', jsonb_build_object(
        'id', new.id,
        'status', new.status,
        'plan_name', new.plan_name
      ),
      -- A resolution is a correction, not news. The sender compares the two
      -- statuses to decide between waking the device (a request appeared) and
      -- quietly updating the count the notification shows.
      'old_record', case
                      when tg_op = 'UPDATE'
                        then jsonb_build_object('status', old.status)
                      else null
                    end
    ),
    headers := jsonb_build_object(
      'Content-Type', 'application/json',
      'x-webhook-secret', v_secret
    ),
    -- Fire and forget: pg_net queues this and makes the call after the
    -- transaction commits, so a slow or unavailable sender never delays the
    -- submission the user is waiting on.
    timeout_milliseconds := 5000
  );

  return null;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.notify_admin_payment_alerts() is
  'Posts {type, table, schema, record, old_record} to the send-admin-payment-alerts Edge Function through pg_net, carrying the Vault-held shared secret. Queues after commit, so it can never delay a payment submission.';

-- Two triggers, not one, because PostgreSQL refuses a WHEN condition that leans
-- on OLD values the moment the same trigger is also an INSERT trigger: "INSERT
-- trigger's WHEN condition cannot reference OLD values" — found by applying this
-- file, not by reading it. The split says the same thing more plainly: a new
-- submission always counts (it is pending by the column's own default), and a
-- status change counts only when the status actually moved, so an unrelated
-- UPDATE — a note being added — sends nothing, because the count it would carry
-- has not changed.
drop trigger if exists trg_payment_submissions_admin_alerts_insert
  on public.payment_submissions;
create trigger trg_payment_submissions_admin_alerts_insert
after insert on public.payment_submissions
for each row
execute function public.notify_admin_payment_alerts();

drop trigger if exists trg_payment_submissions_admin_alerts_update
  on public.payment_submissions;
create trigger trg_payment_submissions_admin_alerts_update
after update on public.payment_submissions
for each row
when (new.status is distinct from old.status)
execute function public.notify_admin_payment_alerts();

revoke all on function public.notify_admin_payment_alerts()
  from public, anon, authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.509 — Task 21 Follow-Up — Five Payment Submissions A Day

### 0.0.509 · `20261013120000_payment_rate_limit_five.sql`

> **Created 2026-10-13.** The daily payment submission limit rises to five: replaces enforce_payment_rate_limit() with the same body and one changed constant (3 -> 5), so the server's BEFORE INSERT gate matches the client's best-effort warning. The trigger, the server-time stamp, the exception and its errcode are unchanged.

```sql
-- Task 21 follow-up — the daily payment submission limit is now five.
--
-- `20260929102100_phase3_sync_and_guard_triggers.sql` set the server-side
-- `BEFORE INSERT` rate limit on `payment_submissions` to three per rolling 24
-- hours (and that value still lives there, as history). The limit is now five,
-- so this replaces `enforce_payment_rate_limit()` with the same body and one
-- changed constant: the count, the server-time stamp, the exception and its
-- errcode are untouched. The trigger already points at the function, so
-- `create or replace` is the whole change and the existing grants carry over.
--
-- The client's best-effort UX copy (`paymentLocalSubmissionLimit`) follows to
-- five in the same phase, so the early warning and the authoritative gate agree.
--
-- Table class: `payment_submissions` remains server-managed (no client write
-- policy exists for it), and this runs as the controlled re-issue of a function
-- under its old grants.

create or replace function public.enforce_payment_rate_limit()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  c_daily_limit constant integer := 5;
  v_recent integer;
begin
  -- Server time only: a client-supplied submitted_at is overwritten, never
  -- trusted, and the rolling count reads this column.
  new.submitted_at := now();

  select pg_catalog.count(*) into v_recent
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.user_id = new.user_id
     and s.submitted_at > now() - interval '24 hours';

  if v_recent >= c_daily_limit then
    raise exception 'payment_submission_limit_reached'
      using errcode = 'P0001',
            hint = 'Five submissions per 24 hours; the client shows the friendly message.';
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;
```
